### Gemini

### Master Lifecycle Role & Cockpit Operational Context

The menu item **[01] BOOT ENGINE & ENSURE SCHEMA** serves as the primary gateway and authoritative cold-start trigger for the `000.series-sower-engine` (also designated `worker-sower-engine`). Rendered within the 12x12 Blessed Curses Terminal Cockpit via `apps/995.library` and `98.menu.unit`, this action represents the sovereign ignition sequence for the entire 24-unit simulation fleet.

When selected by an operator or automation harness, the command delegates execution directly to the Master Lifecycle Orchestrator (`00.engine.unit`). Its mandate is to construct a verified, tamper-proof, and deterministic computational universe from scratch at Stage $T_0 = \$0.00$ before any narrative cycles, conflict lotteries, or mechanical 2d6 duel resolvers are permitted to evaluate. It replaces uncoordinated, race-prone asynchronous startups with an unbreakable, synchronous three-phase state initialization pipeline.

### The Three-Phase Deterministic Boot Sequence

Upon invocation, `00.engine.unit` immediately transitions the global engine lifecycle status from `OFFLINE` to `BOOTING`. It then orchestrates a strict, sequential three-phase cascade via the internal reactive dispatch bus (`ste.hunt`) across dedicated domain units:

#### Phase 1: In-Memory Relational Schema Initialization & DDL Arming

- **Target Subsystem:** `02.database.unit`.
- **Action Flow:** Dispatches the database initialization and schema guarantee actions to mount the local relational ledger.
- **PRAGMA Hardening:** Configures SQLite database pragmas, enforcing strict foreign key constraints, Write-Ahead Logging (WAL) journal mode, normalized synchronous writes, and executing an integrity check to guarantee zero page corruption.
- **Self-Healing Relational DDL:** Bootstraps the full suite of dialect-pure in-memory tables without requiring external migration tooling:
  - `event_log`: The immutable, append-only chronological record containing monotonic sequence identifiers, logical ticks, event types, actor references, payload strings, spatial audit quadruples, previous state hashes, and resulting state hashes.
  - `entities_live`: The somatic and spatial register tracking active coordinates, available arms, available legs, locomotion tiers, concussion levels, basis-point pressure meters, and open wound payloads.
  - `strings_ledger`: The social debt register tracking transactional interpersonal leverage tokens, integer basis-point debt weights, and anti-reflexive counterparty constraints.
  - `grievance_status`: The conflict tracking table recording instigator and victim pairings, cooldown cycle decrements, escalation tree depths, and intensity ratings.
  - `paranoia_clocks`: Doom meters tracking individual and environmental psychological pressure accumulation and boil-over states.
  - `actor_beliefs`: The epistemic horizon register defining localized character perception and enforcing narrative ignorance by omission.
  - `unratified_details`: The quarantined holding ledger for newly emergent narrative facts awaiting consensus ratification.
- **Append-Only Immutability Triggers:** Arms hard database triggers (`trg_event_log_no_update` and `trg_event_log_no_delete`) on `event_log`. Any subsequent attempt to modify or delete committed historical sequence rows immediately aborts execution with an explicit immutability violation error.

#### Phase 2: Primordial Baseline Verification & Genesis Seeding ($S_0$)

- **Target Subsystems:** `13.primordial.unit`, supported by `14.sequence.unit` and `15.series.unit`.
- **Audit of Existing Ledger:** Before applying mutations, the engine performs a count query against `event_log`.
- **Idempotent Preservation:** If existing historical records are detected, the orchestrator preserves the existing timeline, verifying the active sequence cursor and ensuring zero rows are overwritten.
- **Cold-Start Genesis Minting:** If the ledger is clean (holding zero rows), the orchestrator triggers primordial seeding:
  - Ingests the sealed, byte-zero dossier definitions for the active series namespace (such as `under-the-floorboards` or `wiregrass-basin`).
  - Seeds initial somatic baselines into `entities_live`, foundational debts into `strings_ledger`, baseline grievances into `grievance_status`, and initial ticks into `paranoia_clocks`.
  - Constructs and commits Sequence #1 (Genesis Tick $S_0$) directly into `event_log`.
  - Calculates the initial chained event digest against the unseeded baseline genesis hash (a 64-character string of zeroes).
  - Advances the monotonic sequence counter in `14.sequence.unit` strictly to sequence number 1 under Optimistic Concurrency Control.

#### Phase 3: Four-Vector Working State Hydration & Deep Freeze

- **Target Subsystem:** `16.state.unit`.
- **State Assembly:** Scans the newly initialized relational tables and aggregates the four fundamental operational vectors: the somatic vector, the spatial topology vector, the psychological/relational vector, and the chronological event vector.
- **Cryptographic Merkle Root Locking:** Derives the composite state hash (`lastStateHash`) across all active entity leaves using canonical RFC 8785 JSON Canonicalization Scheme (JCS) and SHA-256 digesting.
- **Defensive Immutability Lock:** Applies recursive object freezing to the entire hydrated in-memory state snapshot. This guarantees that no downstream actor models, lottery passes, or external adapters can perform in-place memory mutations.
- **State Finalization:** The master orchestrator records the boot timestamp, binds the active series slug, flips the internal booted indicator to true, and transitions lifecycle status from `BOOTING` to `READY`.

### Constitutional Laws & System Invariants Enforced at Boot

The execution of **[01] BOOT ENGINE & ENSURE SCHEMA** strictly enforces core architectural invariants:

- **Zero Native I/O in Simulation Core:** The orchestrator strictly forbids imports of filesystem modification, child process execution, networking sockets, or native external database drivers within the simulation packages. All table creation and queries operate purely within an isolated in-memory relational port.
- **Fail-Closed Circuit Gating:** If any schema constraint fails, if an unhandled trigger error occurs, or if state hydration yields a corrupted hash, the boot sequence halts immediately. The orchestrator transitions status directly to `ERROR`, flags the state hash as a boot failure, logs diagnostic traces, and refuses to allow downstream story generation.
- **The Gillian Arithmetic Doctrine (Floating-Point Ban):** Ambient floating-point representations are strictly banned across all table definitions, row counts, sequence numbers, timestamps, and basis-point metrics. Every numeric calculation remains pinned to discrete integers and basis points (0 to 10,000).
- **Wall-Clock Temporal Prohibition:** Ambience-derived system clock sampling is strictly banned from simulation buzzers and state reducers. Temporal markers must be explicitly provided via injected integer timestamps and logical tick increments, ensuring 100% bit-level reproducibility across different hardware environments.
- **Hardware Agnosticism & Zero VRAM Footprint:** The entire boot operation runs exclusively in host system RAM on CPU cores (Rig 1), requiring 0 MB of dedicated GPU VRAM.

### Terminal Cockpit Navigation & Real-Time Telemetry

When an operator triggers this menu item within the Blessed TUI, the interface maintains an interactive dual-pane layout with explicit feedback loops:

- **Grid Partitioning:** The interactive selection switchboard remains anchored in the left control columns, while the right eight columns stream real-time receipts into console pane `cns00`.
- **7-Bit Pure ASCII Compliance:** All logging receipts emitted during the three boot phases adhere strictly to standard 7-bit ASCII characters (avoiding multi-byte UTF-8 symbols).
- **Phase Progression Stream:** Telemetry markers explicitly announce stage progression:
  - Emits engine initialization notices certifying that the master orchestrator is active.
  - Streams progress tokens `(1/3)`, `(2/3)`, and `(3/3)` detailing SQLite schema verification, primordial seeding status, and deep-frozen state hydration.
  - Outputs the active head sequence number and truncated 64-character SHA-256 state hash receipt upon successful transition to `READY`.
- **Headless Fallback Compatibility:** When invoked inside non-interactive automated test suites or headless Continuous Integration environments where the terminal UI is absent, all underlying buzzers and action handlers resolve through promise resolvers with zero standard I/O buffer collisions.

### Gemini

The simulation engine does not load raw Markdown files directly from disk at runtime; rather, **the starter dossiers are authored and compiled upstream in the legislative canon repository, and the engine ingests the resulting sealed artifact through a shared client package**.

### 1. Where the Authoritative Byte-Zero Dossiers Live on Disk

The raw Markdown dossiers that define a story world reside upstream in the legislative canon repository (**`001.goblin-lore`** or its franchise sibling **`wiregrass-lore`**, located under the **`01.canon/`** cluster folder in the master workspace root `FLEET_ROOT`):

```plaintext
01.canon/001.goblin-lore/ (or wiregrass-lore/)
└── series/<series-slug>/                  # e.g., series/under-the-floorboards/ or series/wiregrass-basin/
    ├── series.config.json                 # Series axioms, pacing parameters, custom enums
    ├── characters/
    │   └── char_protagonist.md            # (e.g., bog.md or char_protagonist.md) Byte-0 frontmatter
    ├── locations/
    │   └── loc_origin_hub.md              # Staging node, acoustic damping, spatial coordinates
    ├── possessions/
    │   └── item_founder_token.md          # EquipSlot and weight class definitions
    ├── grievances/
    │   └── grv_territorial_impasse.md     # Dual-Rail participant, location, prop anchor DAG
    └── ontology/
        └── unresolved.json                # Empty/starter mystery registry
```

Every starter file in these subdirectories satisfies the **Byte-0 Offset Invariant** (the file physically starts at byte index 0 with `---` / `0x2D 0x2D 0x2D`, without UTF-8 BOM, leading newlines, or spaces).

### 2. How the Seed Files Are Scaffolded & Provisioned

When a new series is created or starter dossiers are emitted, the generation is handled by domain buzzers in **`packages/299.lore`** (or **`packages/001.lore`**) inside the canon repository:

- **Tree Provisioner:** `packages/299.lore/29.series.unit/buz/scaffoldSeriesTree.buzz.ts` creates the directory tree (`characters/`, `locations/`, `possessions/`, `grievances/`, `ontology/`) and the target `compiled/<slug>/` folder.
- **Location Starter:** `packages/299.lore/27.seed.unit/buz/seedOriginHub.buzz.ts` (or `01.location.unit`) writes `loc_origin_hub.md`.
- **Possession Starter:** `packages/299.lore/27.seed.unit/buz/seedFounderToken.buzz.ts` (or `12.gear.unit`) writes `item_founder_token.md`.
- **Protagonist Starter:** Reuses `packages/299.lore/02.character.unit/buz/seedProtagonist.buzz.ts` to emit `char_protagonist.md`.
- **Grievance Starter:** `packages/299.lore/27.seed.unit/buz/seedTerritorialImpasse.buzz.ts` (or `13.grievance.unit`) writes `grv_territorial_impasse.md`.
- **Batch Orchestrator:** `packages/299.lore/27.seed.unit/buz/orchestrateGenesisSeeds.buzz.ts` coordinates writing all four starter dossiers in a single deterministic pass.

### 3. Upstream Compilation into the Sealed State Artifact

The simulation core enforces a strict **Zero Runtime Markdown Parsing** invariant. The raw Markdown dossiers are evaluated offline by the 3-Pass Canon Compiler (`packages/299.lore/41.canon.unit/` or `packages/001.lore/src/compiler.ts`):

1. **Pass 1 (Shape & Scalar):** Verifies Byte-0 headers and validates YAML frontmatter against strict Zod contracts.
2. **Pass 2 (Graph & Cycles):** Verifies relational pointers (character to location/possession) and runs a 3-color DFS to guarantee acyclic grievance trees.
3. **Pass 3 (Deterministic Seal):** Sorts keys lexicographically (RFC 8785 JCS) and calculates the 64-character SHA-256 Merkle root ($H_{\text{root}}$).

The compiler atomically writes the sealed output to disk at:

```plaintext
01.canon/001.goblin-lore/compiled/<series-slug>/bible-state.json
```

(with an immutable timestamped copy `bible-state_<TIMESTAMP>.json` alongside it).

### 4. How `000.series-sower-engine` Ingests It at Boot

In **`000.series-sower-engine`** (located at `02.simulation/000.series-sower-engine/`), the boot sequence ingests the compiled data through the following chain:

1. **Dependency Link:** The engine's `package.json` links the canon package locally:
  ```json
  {
    "dependencies": {
      "@camp_candor/001.lore": "file:../../01.canon/001.goblin-lore/packages/001.lore"
    }
  }
  ```
2. **Typed Client Accessor:** Ingestion occurs exclusively through `packages/001.lore/src/client.ts` via the `loadBibleState('<series-slug>')` function. It reads `compiled/<series-slug>/bible-state.json`, checks $H_{\text{root}}$, and wraps the entire payload in recursive `Object.freeze()`.
3. **Genesis Seeding ($S_0$):** `15.series.unit` passes this validated payload to `13.primordial.unit` and `02.database.unit`, which unpack the compiled entity records (characters, locations, debts, grievances) directly into the in-memory SQLite tables (`entities_live`, `strings_ledger`, `grievance_status`, `paranoia_clocks`) to mint Sequence #1.

### 3. Update the Menu Logic in `packages/300.engine/98.menu.unit/buz/00.menu.buzz.ts`

When `[01] BOOT ENGINE & ENSURE SCHEMA` is selected, intercept the execution to:

3. Pass the selected world slug into `ActEng.BOOT_ENGINE`.

#### Modified `handleMenuSelection` in `00.menu.buzz.ts`

```typescript
import fs from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';
import * as ActEng from '../../00.engine.unit/engine.action.js';
import type State from '../../99.core/state.js';

// Helper to resolve the engine root directory
function resolveEngineRoot(): string {
  let curr = process.cwd();
  while (curr && curr !== path.dirname(curr)) {
    if (
      fs.existsSync(path.join(curr, 'compiled')) ||
      (fs.existsSync(path.join(curr, 'packages', '300.engine')))
    ) {
      return curr;
    }
    curr = path.dirname(curr);
  }
  return process.cwd();
}

export const handleMenuSelection = async (
  selection: string,
  ste?: State,
): Promise<void> => {
  if (!ste || typeof ste.hunt !== 'function') {
    return;
  }

  const lib =
    (globalThis as any).LIBRARY ||
    (typeof global !== 'undefined' ? (global as any).LIBRARY : undefined);

  switch (selection) {
    case '[01] BOOT ENGINE & ENSURE SCHEMA': {
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [BOOT] Scanning compiled/ directory for world instances...');

      const engineRoot = resolveEngineRoot();
      const compiledDir = path.resolve(engineRoot, 'compiled');
      let worldChoices: string[] = [];

      if (fs.existsSync(compiledDir)) {
        worldChoices = fs
          .readdirSync(compiledDir, { withFileTypes: true })
          .filter(
            (dirent) =>
              dirent.isDirectory() &&
              !dirent.name.startsWith('.') &&
              !dirent.name.startsWith('_') &&
              fs.existsSync(path.join(compiledDir, dirent.name, 'bible-state.json'))
          )
          .map((dirent) => dirent.name);
      }

      if (worldChoices.length === 0) {
        await emitConsole(ste, ':: [ERROR] No compiled worlds found in compiled/.');
        await emitConsole(ste, ':: Please copy compiled worlds from 000.goblin-lore/compiled/.');
        break;
      }

      let selectedSlug: string = worldChoices[0];

      // If interactive Blessed TUI is mounted, render selection sub-menu
      if (lib && typeof lib.hunt === 'function') {
        const subList = [...worldChoices, 'CANCEL'];

        const subGrid = await lib.hunt('[Grid action] Update Grid', {
          x: 0,
          y: 4,
          xSpan: 4,
          ySpan: Math.min(12, Math.max(6, subList.length + 2)),
        });

        const choiceRes = await lib.hunt('[Open action] Open Choice', {
          dat: { clr0: 'black', clr1: 'green' },
          src: 'vertical',
          lst: subList,
          net: subGrid?.grdBit?.dat,
        });

        const picked = choiceRes?.chcBit?.src;
        if (!picked || picked === 'CANCEL') {
          await emitConsole(ste, '>> [BOOT] Engine boot canceled by operator.');
          break;
        }
        selectedSlug = picked;
      }

      await emitConsole(ste, `>> [BOOT] Ingesting compiled state for: [${selectedSlug}]`);

      // Dispatch boot action with selected series slug
      await ste.hunt(ActEng.BOOT_ENGINE, {
        dat: {
          bootParams: {
            seriesSlug: selectedSlug,
            seedGenesis: true,
          },
        },
      });
      break;
    }

    // ... other menu cases remain unchanged
  }
};
```

### 4. Update the Ingestion Loader in `packages/300.engine/00.engine.unit/buz/engine.buzz.ts`

Ensure `bootEngine` reads from `./compiled/<seriesSlug>/bible-state.json` instead of expecting a hardcoded slug or an external mock:

```typescript
import fs from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';

export const bootEngine = async (cpy: any, bal: any, ste: any) => {
  const seriesSlug = bal?.dat?.bootParams?.seriesSlug || 'wiregrass-basin';
  const compiledPath = path.resolve(process.cwd(), 'compiled', seriesSlug, 'bible-state.json');

  if (!fs.existsSync(compiledPath)) {
    throw new Error(`[BOOT_FAILURE] bible-state.json missing at: ${compiledPath}`);
  }

  // Read and parse compiled world law
  const rawState = fs.readFileSync(compiledPath, 'utf-8');
  const bibleState = Object.freeze(JSON.parse(rawState));

  // Initialize SQLite DDL and triggers
  await ste.hunt('[Database action] Init Database', {});
  await ste.hunt('[Database action] Ensure Schema', {});

  // Seed Genesis S_0 from parsed bibleState
  await ste.hunt('[Primordial action] Seed Primordial', {
    dat: {
      seriesSlug,
      bibleState,
    },
  });

  // Hydrate Four-Vector working state and lock Merkle root
  await ste.hunt('[State action] Hydrate State', {
    dat: { seriesSlug },
  });

  return cpy;
};
```

### You

is there one SQLite database for the entire engine or will there be a sperate Database for each compiled world; no code only cover the concepts

### Gemini

The engine uses a **separate SQLite database for each compiled world**, rather than a single monolithic database.

This partitioning is enforced through the system's namespace isolation model, ledger lifecycle, and data integrity boundaries:

### 1. Isolated File Routing per Series Slug

When a compiled world is selected and initialized, the series manager subsystem (`15.series.unit`) resolves a dedicated, slug-partitioned SQLite storage target:

$$
\text{Active Series Slug} \longrightarrow \text{data/sqlite/}\langle\text{series-slug}\rangle\text{.local.db}
$$

- Selecting `wiregrass-basin` mounts and routes writes strictly to `data/sqlite/wiregrass-basin.local.db`.
- Selecting `blesssed-and-the-bounded` routes writes to `data/sqlite/blesssed-and-the-bounded.local.db`.
- In-memory test passes isolate execution to transient, independent `:memory:` handles so that test suites run with zero shared persistence.

The database port (`02.database.unit`) accepts this dynamic target path during the boot sequence, disconnecting any previous database handle and opening the dedicated file for the chosen world.

### 2. Independent Monotonic Event Logs & Merkle Anchors

The simulation engine operates on an event-sourced ledger model where every state mutation is recorded in an append-only log (`event_log`). Merging multiple worlds into a single database file would corrupt the mathematical foundation of the simulation:

- **Monotonic Sequence Continuity:** Each world maintains its own unbroken, gapless sequence counter ($seq_{n+1} = seq_n + 1$) starting at Genesis sequence #1 ($S_0$). A shared database would either cause sequence collisions or force arbitrary interleaving of events from completely unrelated story universes.
- **Chained Hash Integrity:** Every event envelope cryptographically seals the previous event hash into its resulting state hash ($H_n = \text{SHA-256}(H_{n-1} \parallel \dots)$). Because hash chains are anchored to a world’s unique Genesis Merkle root ($H_{\text{root}}$), each world must live in an isolated ledger to preserve verifiable, bit-exact replayability.

### 3. Containment of Immutability Triggers & Cascades

The SQLite database layer deploys hard relational triggers to prevent historical tampering and automate staleness tracking:

- `trg_event_log_no_update` and `trg_event_log_no_delete` abort any query that attempts to modify committed history.
- Reactive invalidation triggers (`trg_fact_version_bump`) compare fact version counters and mark dependent scenes as dirty.

Maintaining separate physical database files ensures that relational triggers, foreign key constraints, and vacuum/checkpoint maintenance in one story world never cascade into or lock the state of another.

### 4. Preservation of the Deletion and Export Invariant

By keeping a 1:1 parity between a compiled world folder (`compiled/<slug>/`) and its local database file (`data/sqlite/<slug>.local.db`), each story world forms an autonomous container:

- **Zero Cross-World Contamination:** No entity, location, or leverage string can create dangling foreign-key references to an entity from a different franchise.
- **Clean Archival & Export:** An entire narrative timeline can be exported to canonical format, archived, or wiped clean without affecting other running or paused simulations in the engine.

### Feature Identification & Architectural Framing

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     SOVEREIGN SEEDING & ISOLATION                        │
├───────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Formal Feature Title              │ CANON AIRLOCK & DISCRETE REALM VAULT │
├───────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Architectural Epic Nomenclature   │ EPIC-08: AXIOMATIC GENESIS &         │
│                                   │          COMPARTMENTALIZED LEDGERS   │
├───────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Cockpit Switchboard Route         │ [01] BOOT ENGINE & ENSURE SCHEMA     │
├───────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Target Control Plane              │ packages/300.engine/98.menu.unit     │
├───────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Purity Enforcement Boundary       │ ZERO NATIVE I/O IN SIMULATION CORE   │
└───────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Separating these operations into two distinct features enforces the purity constraints of the architecture:

1. **Axiomatic World Discovery & Air-Gapped Ingestion (The "Canon Airlock"):** Keeps disk operations, file enumeration, JSON deserialization, and interactive Blessed TUI selection strictly quarantined inside the shell layer (`98.menu.unit` and `apps/995.library`). The core simulation package (`packages/300.engine/00.engine.unit` through `24.unit`) receives a fully materialized, read-only, in-memory Genesis payload via reactive bus dispatch (`ste.hunt`). This leaves the `check:native-io` tripwire active across the entire simulation kernel without requiring bypasses or lint exceptions.
2. **Compartmentalized Single-Universe Relational Persistence (The "Discrete Realm Vault"):** Guarantees that each selected world slug resolves to its own autonomous SQLite database file (`data/sqlite/<series-slug>.local.db`). Monotonic sequence counters, append-only immutability triggers (`trg_event_log_no_update`), and chained cryptographic hashes ($H_0 \to H_{\text{root}}$) remain strictly isolated to that specific universe.

# Blueprint: EPIC-08: AXIOMATIC GENESIS & COMPARTMENTALIZED LEDGERS

## Repository: `000.series-sower-engine`

### Executive Overview & Architectural Invariants

This blueprint operationalizes **EPIC-08: AXIOMATIC GENESIS & COMPARTMENTALIZED LEDGERS** within `000.series-sower-engine`. It establishes a strict physical separation between the host terminal file airlock and the zero-I/O simulation core:

1. **The Canon Airlock (`98.menu.unit`):** Scans the local `./compiled/` directory for pre-compiled world artifacts (`compiled/<series-slug>/bible-state.json`), renders a dynamic Blessed TUI choice menu, deserializes the JSON manifest, locks it defensively with `Object.freeze()`, and forwards the in-memory object through the reactive BehaviorSubject event bus (`ste.hunt`).
2. **The Zero-I/O Simulation Kernel (`00.engine.unit` through `16.state.unit`):** Receives the frozen payload in host RAM. The `check:native-io` boundary remains 100% intact: the kernel performs zero filesystem reads, child process spawns, or network calls.
3. **The Discrete Realm Vault (`02.database.unit` & `15.series.unit`):** Automatically partitions persistent state by routing each series slug to its own dedicated SQLite database (`data/sqlite/<series-slug>.local.db`). Each realm operates an independent monotonic sequence counter ($seq_{n+1} = seq_n + 1$) starting at Genesis sequence #1 ($S_0$).
4. **Local Development First, Cloudflare Edge Second:** Full local orchestration, schema bootstrapping, and Blessed terminal controls run locally before synchronizing into `apps/worker` for edge isolate execution.

    1. `event_log`: Monotonic sequence counter, event IDs, ticks, state hashes, and spatial audit quadruples.
    2. `entities_live`: Coordinates $(q, r)$, arm/leg counts, locomotion tiers, concussion levels, wounds, and inventory JSON arrays.

  - Execute DDL for the 3 continuity tables: `facts`, `scenes`, and `scene_fact_dependencies`.

  - Gillian Arithmetic Doctrine: floating-point column types are banned; all metrics are stored as discrete integers or basis points ($0\text{--}10{,}000\text{ bps}$).

- **Target Files:**

    - `trg_event_log_no_delete`: Blocks any `DELETE` statement on `event_log` with an explicit `IMMUTABILITY_VIOLATION` error.
  - Arm the monotonic version trigger on `facts`:
    - `trg_fact_version_bump`: Asserts `NEW.version > OLD.version` and cascades `is_dirty = 1` to dependent rows in `scenes`.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - Historical permanence: once Sequence #1 is written, past events cannot be mutated or deleted.

### Gemini

### The Autonomous Operational Loop: Running the Clockwork Simulation

Once **[01] BOOT ENGINE & ENSURE SCHEMA** completes and your compiled world is selected, the engine transitions from cold-start setup into its active lifecycle:

```
[01] BOOT ENGINE ──► [READY] ──► SELECT RUN MODE ──► ADVANCE TICKS ──► DRAMATIC HALT ──► DRAFT/MUTATE ──► RESUME
```

Running the simulation involves executing the **"Wind-Up Clockwork Machine"**: the engine runs forward tick-by-tick at CPU memory speed, trips an automatic brake when physical and interpersonal tension boil over, resolves conflict outcomes through mechanical dice rules, pauses for scene drafting, measures duration into elapsed ticks, and resumes forward progression.

### Step 1: Initiating Execution from the Terminal Cockpit

With the engine in `READY` status, navigation switches from boot controls to runtime dispatch in the left control panel (`98.menu.unit`):

#### Option A: Interactive Step Execution (Single-Tick & Tactical Inspection)

For manual inspection and stepping through specific state transitions:

1. **Highlight `[14] EXECUTE DETERMINISTIC` (or `ADVANCE TICK`):**
  - Dispatches `TICK_ADVANCED(n)` into the event pipeline.

  >> [TICK:DECAY_SWEEP] [OK] Decayed 2 clocks, updated 1 grievance.
  >> [STATE:UPDATE] Paranoia baseline: 2450 bps (-50 bps).

#### Option B: Automated Fast-Forward / Batch Gauntlet Run

For running unconstrained continuous cycles:

1. **Highlight `RUN SIM GAUNTLET` (or dispatching the batch run harness via `apps/local-runner/src/main.ts`):**
  - Ingests the configured cycle count (e.g., 100, 1,000, or 10,000 ticks).
  - Executes purely in host RAM at microsecond speeds, running spatial hash sweeps and mathematical vector additions across all active entities.
  - Runs in **`SIMULATION_ONLY`** mode, advancing world history without requiring GPU rendering hardware.

### Step 2: Spatial Queries & Conflict Selection (Stage 3)

As ticks advance, the simulation core evaluates whether entities collide:

1. **Local Scene Hot-Set Extraction:**
  - Uses bit-packed axial coordinates $\text{packAxial}(q, r)$ to sweep the hex grid, grouping collocated or adjacent characters ($d_{\text{axial}} \le 1$) into an active hot-set.
2. **The "Boil-Over" Hard Override:**
  - Before running general lotteries, the engine queries `grievance_status` and `paranoia_clocks`.
  - If the resentment accumulator or paranoia meter between two characters breaches its maximum threshold ($I_{\text{curr}} \ge I_{\text{max}}$), the system triggers an immediate confrontation override, preventing grudges from simmering indefinitely.
3. **The Dyadic Encounter Lottery:**
  - If no override trips, the Sower evaluates all pairings combinatorially ($\frac{N(N-1)}{2}$) using a deterministic PRNG lottery weighted by physical distance, debt strings held, and unspent grievance intensity.

### Step 3: Mechanical Stakes Resolution (Stage 4 Opposed Duel)

When a confrontation triggers, the engine trips its clock brake: **the timeline freezes at Tick $t$, and mechanics determine reality before any dialogue is authored**:

1. **Opposed Tabletop Resolution:**
  - Evaluates the conflict:
    $$
    \text{Outcome} = 2d6_{\text{opposed}} + \text{Leverage Strings} - \text{Somatic Debuffs}
    $$
  - Categorizes the result into a discrete mechanical tier (`DECISIVE_WIN`, `COSTLY_WIN`, `STANDOFF`, or `HUMILIATING_LOSS`).
2. **Intermediate Contract Emission:**
  - Compiles the resolution into an immutable **`ScenePlan IR`**.
  - Mandates the physical exit conditions (e.g., character surrenders an item, suffers a dislocated shoulder, or flees the room).

### Step 4: The Authoring Pause & Time Quantization

With the world frozen at Tick $t$, the narrative event is dramatized:

1. **Screenplay Ingress in `drafts/`:**
  - Mounts the scene workspace in `000.series-sower-engine/drafts/` (or the Director's Writer Desk).
  - The human showrunner or local drafting agent authors the scene dialogue in `.fountain` format to fit the mandated mechanical exit conditions.
  - **Somatic SAT & Epistemic Filters:** Enforces that characters cannot use paralyzed limbs or reference facts they have not observed (Ignorance by Omission).
2. **The Acoustic Clock Quantization:**
  - Spoken lines are synthesized into speech stems via the local audio microservice (`:8000`).
  - Audio duration is measured down to the millisecond using `ffprobe` and converted into exact simulation frames and ticks:
    $$
    \Delta_{\text{ticks}} = \left\lceil \frac{\text{Duration}_{\text{ms}}}{1000 \times \text{TickInterval}} \right\rceil
    $$

### Step 5: State Settlement & Resuming the Timeline

1. **Atomic Mutation Commit:**
  - State changes (injuries debited to `entities_live`, tokens shifted in `strings_ledger`, cooldowns reset in `grievance_status`) are committed in an atomic transaction to the active world database (`data/sqlite/<series-slug>.local.db`).
  - The cryptographic Merkle state hash updates:
    $$
    H_{t+1} = \text{SHA-256}(H_t \parallel \text{EventData})
    $$
2. **Release the Brake:**
  - The master clock advances by the elapsed scene duration ($t \leftarrow t + \Delta_{\text{ticks}}$).
  - Unobserved background sites apply closed-form algebraic catch-up formulas.
  - The simulation resumes autonomous ticking until the next dramatic threshold is breached.

import fs from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';
import * as ActEng from '../../00.engine.unit/engine.action.js';
import * as ActMnu from '../menu.action.js';
import type { MenuModel } from '../menu.model.js';
import type MenuBit from '../fce/menu.bit.js';
import type State from '../../99.core/state.js';

let rootSlv: any = null;

const UPDATE_GRID = '[Grid action] Update Grid';
const WRITE_CONSOLE = '[Write action] Write Console';
const UPDATE_CONSOLE = '[Console action] Update Console';
const OPEN_CHOICE = '[Open action] Open Choice';

const emitConsole = async (ste: State | undefined, src: string): Promise<void> => {
  const lib = (globalThis as any).LIBRARY || (global as any).LIBRARY;
  if (lib && typeof lib.hunt === 'function') {
    try {
      await lib.hunt(UPDATE_CONSOLE, { idx: 'cns00', src });
      return;
    } catch {
      // Fall through to standard out in headless environments
    }
  }
  if (ste && typeof ste.hunt === 'function') {
    try {
      await ste.hunt(UPDATE_CONSOLE, { idx: 'cns00', src });
      return;
    } catch {
      // Degrade gracefully
    }
  }
};

const resolveEngineRoot = (): string => {
  let curr = process.cwd();
  while (curr && curr !== path.dirname(curr)) {
    if (
      fs.existsSync(path.join(curr, 'compiled')) ||
      fs.existsSync(path.join(curr, 'packages', '300.engine'))
    ) {
      return curr;
    }
    curr = path.dirname(curr);
  }
  return process.cwd();
};

const scanCompiledWorlds = (): string[] => {
  try {
    const engineRoot = resolveEngineRoot();
    const compiledDir = path.resolve(engineRoot, 'compiled');
    if (!fs.existsSync(compiledDir)) return [];

    return fs
      .readdirSync(compiledDir, { withFileTypes: true })
      .filter(
        (dirent) =>
          dirent.isDirectory() &&
          !dirent.name.startsWith('.') &&
          !dirent.name.startsWith('_') &&
          fs.existsSync(path.join(compiledDir, dirent.name, 'bible-state.json')),
      )
      .map((dirent) => dirent.name);
  } catch {
    return [];
  }
};

const deepFreeze = <T>(obj: T): T => {
  if (obj === null || typeof obj !== 'object') return obj;
  Object.freeze(obj);
  for (const key of Object.getOwnPropertyNames(obj)) {
    const val = (obj as any)[key];
    if (val !== null && typeof val === 'object' && !Object.isFrozen(val)) {
      deepFreeze(val);
    }
  }
  return obj;
};

export const initMenu = async (
  cpy: MenuModel,
  bal?: MenuBit,
  ste?: State,
): Promise<MenuModel> => {
  cpy.activeSelection = '';
  cpy.lastActionTimestamp = Date.now();
  if (bal?.slv != null) rootSlv = bal.slv;

  const lib = (globalThis as any).LIBRARY || (global as any).LIBRARY;
  if (!lib || typeof lib.hunt !== 'function') {
    cpy.isHeadless = true;
    if (bal?.slv) {
      bal.slv({ mnuBit: { idx: 'init-menu-headless-pass', val: 1, src: 'HEADLESS' } });
    }
    return cpy;
  }

  cpy.isHeadless = false;

  let bit = await lib.hunt(UPDATE_GRID, {
    x: 4,
    y: 0,
    xSpan: 8,
    ySpan: 12,
  });
  await lib.hunt(WRITE_CONSOLE, {
    idx: 'cns00',
    src: '',
    dat: { net: bit?.grdBit?.dat, src: 'engine0' },
  });

  await emitConsole(ste, '------------------------------------------------------------');
  await emitConsole(ste, '>> [FLIGHT_DECK:READY] 300.ENGINE SIMULATION COCKPIT ONLINE [OK]');
  await emitConsole(ste, '>> [BEE:MANIFEST] 25 Sovereign Units Synchronized & Online');
  await emitConsole(ste, '>> [OK] packages/300.engine active in local isolate (Zero VRAM)');
  await emitConsole(ste, '------------------------------------------------------------');

  return updateMenu(cpy, { ...bal, slv: undefined } as MenuBit, ste).then(() => cpy);
};

export const updateMenu = async (
  cpy: MenuModel,
  bal?: MenuBit,
  ste?: State,
): Promise<MenuModel> => {
  const lib = (globalThis as any).LIBRARY || (global as any).LIBRARY;
  const isHeadless = !lib || typeof lib.hunt !== 'function';

  // Branch menu options based on engine flightDeckMode
  const isRuntime = cpy.flightDeckMode === 'RUNTIME';

  const bootList = [
    '[01] BOOT ENGINE & ENSURE SCHEMA',
    '[02] STATUS ENGINE & TABLE',
    '[03] AUDIT SIMULATION INVARIANTS',
    '[04] RESET ENGINE TO OFFLINE',
    '[ROOT MENU]',
  ];

  const runtimeList = [
    '[14] EXECUTE DETERMINISTIC',
    'ADVANCE TICK',
    'RUN SIM GAUNTLET',
    '[02] STATUS ENGINE & TABLE',
    '[03] AUDIT SIMULATION INVARIANTS',
    '[04] RESET ENGINE TO OFFLINE',
    '[ROOT MENU]',
  ];

  const lst = isRuntime ? runtimeList : bootList;

  if (isHeadless) {
    if (bal?.slv) {
      bal.slv({
        mnuBit: {
          idx: 'update-menu-headless',
          val: 1,
          src: isRuntime ? 'RUNTIME_MODE' : 'BOOT_MODE',
        },
      });
    }
    return cpy;
  }

  const bit = await lib.hunt(UPDATE_GRID, {
    x: 0,
    y: 4,
    xSpan: 4,
    ySpan: Math.min(12, Math.max(6, lst.length + 2)),
  });

  const choiceBit = await lib.hunt(OPEN_CHOICE, {
    dat: { clr0: 'black', clr1: isRuntime ? 'cyan' : 'green' },
    src: 'vertical',
    lst,
    net: bit?.grdBit?.dat,
  });

  const picked = choiceBit?.chcBit?.src;
  cpy.activeSelection = picked || '';
  cpy.lastActionTimestamp = Date.now();

  switch (picked) {
    case '[01] BOOT ENGINE & ENSURE SCHEMA': {
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [AIRLOCK::SCAN] Sweeping compiled/ universe manifests from host volume...');
      const worlds = scanCompiledWorlds();

      if (worlds.length === 0) {
        await emitConsole(ste, ':: [ERROR] No compiled worlds found in compiled/.');
        await emitConsole(ste, ':: Please compile a series in 000.goblin-lore before booting.');
        break;
      }

      let selectedSlug = worlds[0];

      if (worlds.length > 1) {
        const subList = [...worlds, 'CANCEL'];
        const subGrid = await lib.hunt(UPDATE_GRID, {
          x: 0,
          y: 4,
          xSpan: 4,
          ySpan: Math.min(12, Math.max(6, subList.length + 2)),
        });

        const choiceRes = await lib.hunt(OPEN_CHOICE, {
          dat: { clr0: 'black', clr1: 'yellow' },
          src: 'vertical',
          lst: subList,
          net: subGrid?.grdBit?.dat,
        });

        const chosenWorld = choiceRes?.chcBit?.src;
        if (!chosenWorld || chosenWorld === 'CANCEL') {
          await emitConsole(ste, '>> [AIRLOCK::ABORT] Ingestion cancelled by operator.');
          break;
        }
        selectedSlug = chosenWorld;
      }

      await emitConsole(ste, `>> [AIRLOCK::INGEST] Target selected: [${selectedSlug}]`);
      const engineRoot = resolveEngineRoot();
      const manifestPath = path.resolve(engineRoot, 'compiled', selectedSlug, 'bible-state.json');
      const rawManifest = fs.readFileSync(manifestPath, 'utf8');
      const parsedManifest = deepFreeze(JSON.parse(rawManifest));

      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [AIRLOCK::SEAL] In-memory defensive lock applied (Object.freeze)');
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [AIRLOCK::PURITY] Zero Native I/O Fence verified: 0 disk handles forwarded');

      if (ste && typeof ste.hunt === 'function') {
        const bootRes: any = await ste.hunt(ActEng.BOOT_ENGINE, {
          dat: {
            bootParams: {
              seriesSlug: selectedSlug,
              bibleState: parsedManifest,
              seedGenesis: true,
            },
          },
        });

        // Switch to RUNTIME mode upon successful engine boot
        cpy.flightDeckMode = 'RUNTIME';
        cpy.activeSeriesSlug = selectedSlug;

        await emitConsole(ste, '------------------------------------------------------------');
        await emitConsole(ste, '>> [EPIC-08] AXIOMATIC GENESIS & COMPARTMENTALIZED LEDGERS [ACTIVE]');
        await emitConsole(ste, `>> [RUNTIME:READY] Active Universe: [${selectedSlug}]`);
        await emitConsole(ste, '>> [SWITCHBOARD] Navigation switched to RUNTIME DISPATCH in left deck.');
        await emitConsole(ste, '------------------------------------------------------------');
      }
      break;
    }

    case '[14] EXECUTE DETERMINISTIC': {
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [EXECUTE] Dispatching Stage 3 Deterministic Encounter Lottery...');
      if (ste && typeof ste.hunt === 'function') {
        await ste.hunt(ActEng.STATUS_ENGINE, {});
      }
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [EXECUTE:OK] Deterministic stage evaluation complete.');
      break;
    }

    case 'ADVANCE TICK': {
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [TICK] Advancing logical clock (+1 tick) & executing decay sweep...');
      if (ste && typeof ste.hunt === 'function') {
        // Dispatches to 17.tick.unit and 12.paranoia.unit
        await ste.hunt('[Tick action] Advance Tick', { val: 1 });
      }
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [TICK:OK] Clock advanced. Doom meters decayed.');
      break;
    }

    case 'RUN SIM GAUNTLET': {
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [GAUNTLET] Launching continuous simulation gauntlet (100 ticks)...');
      if (ste && typeof ste.hunt === 'function') {
        for (let i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
          await ste.hunt('[Tick action] Advance Tick', { val: 20 });
        }
      }
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [GAUNTLET:COMPLETE] 100 ticks evaluated in host RAM (0 MB VRAM).');
      break;
    }

    case '[02] STATUS ENGINE & TABLE': {
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [STATUS] Querying 4-Vector table status and row inventories...');
      if (ste && typeof ste.hunt === 'function') {
        await ste.hunt(ActEng.STATUS_ENGINE, {});
      }
      break;
    }

    case '[03] AUDIT SIMULATION INVARIANTS': {
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [AUDIT] Running non-destructive T_0 boundary probe...');
      if (ste && typeof ste.hunt === 'function') {
        await ste.hunt(ActEng.AUDIT_ENGINE, {});
      }
      break;
    }

    case '[04] RESET ENGINE TO OFFLINE': {
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [RESET] Resetting simulation engine to OFFLINE status...');
      if (ste && typeof ste.hunt === 'function') {
        await ste.hunt(ActEng.RESET_ENGINE, {});
      }
      // Revert menu flightDeckMode back to BOOT
      cpy.flightDeckMode = 'BOOT';
      cpy.activeSeriesSlug = '';
      await emitConsole(ste, '>> [RESET:OK] Cockpit returned to BOOT & ENSURE SCHEMA mode.');
      break;
    }

    case '[ROOT MENU]': {
      if (rootSlv != null) {
        rootSlv({ mnuBit: { idx: 'root-menu' } });
      }
      return cpy;
    }
  }

  // Non-recursive re-entrant loop yield
  setTimeout(async () => {
    if (ste && typeof ste.hunt === 'function') {
      await ste.hunt(ActMnu.UPDATE_MENU, {});
    }
  }, 333);

  return cpy;
};

export const switchFlightDeckMode = async (
  cpy: MenuModel,
  bal?: MenuBit,
  ste?: State,
): Promise<MenuModel> => {
  if (bal?.dat?.flightDeckMode) {
    cpy.flightDeckMode = bal.dat.flightDeckMode;
  }
  if (bal?.slv) {
    bal.slv({
      mnuBit: {
        idx: 'switch-flight-deck-mode',
        src: cpy.flightDeckMode,
      },
    });
  }
  return cpy;
};

import clone from 'clone-deep';

import { MenuModel } from './menu.model.js';
import * as Buzz from './menu.buzzer.js';
import type State from '../99.core/state.js';

export function reducer(
  model: MenuModel = new MenuModel(),
  act: Act.Actions,
  state?: State,
) {
  switch (act.type) {
    case Act.INIT_MENU:
      return Buzz.initMenu(clone(model), act.bale, state);

    case Act.UPDATE_MENU:
      return Buzz.updateMenu(clone(model), act.bale, state);

    case Act.SWITCH_FLIGHT_DECK_MODE:
      return Buzz.switchFlightDeckMode(clone(model), act.bale, state);

    default:
      return model;
  }
}
```

### Gemini

### The Constitutional Mandate: Simulation Precedes Prose

Running the simulation does not generate a screenplay by dispatching open-ended prompts to an ungrounded large language model. Instead, the architecture enforces an absolute constitutional law: **simulation determines what happens; the language model determines how it is said; the renderer determines how it is seen and heard**.

In this system, screenwriting is not treated as freeform invention, but as an industrial compilation step operating at the boundary between **Axis A (Semantic Truth & World Law)** and **Axis B (Physical Representation & Media Projection)**. Narrative reality—including which actors are present, the exact grudges driving them, who wins physical or social confrontations, what bodily trauma is sustained, and what possessions change hands—is mathematically settled in an immutable relational ledger *before* a writer touches a keyboard or a generative model outputs a single token of dialogue.

### The Seven-Phase Operational Lifecycle: From Tick to Screenplay

The transition from an active simulation tick into a finished screenplay occurs through a continuous, closed-loop machine cycle:

```plaintext
 1. AUTONOMOUS RUN          Simulation advances ticks at CPU memory speed;
                            paranoia meters, hunger, and grievances accumulate.
                                         │
                                         ▼
 2. THE DRAMATIC HALT       Spatial proximity (d_axial <= 1) and tension ceiling
                            breach trigger the clock brake; world freezes at Tick t.
                                         │
                                         ▼
 3. MECHANICAL RESOLUTION   Stage 4 Opposed 2d6 Duel evaluates stakes;
                            emits intermediate contract: ScenePlan IR.
                                         │
                                         ▼
 4. CONSTRAINED DRAFTING    Mounts writer workspace; injects Spatial Stage Header;
                            human or local model drafts prose to fit mandated exit.
                                         │
                                         ▼
 5. INVARIANT GAUNTLET      T_0 = $0.00 audit: Somatic SAT, Banned Kinetic Verbs,
                            and Epistemic Horizon (Ignorance by Omission).
                                         │
                                         ▼
 6. ACOUSTIC QUANTIZATION   TTS synthesizes 48 kHz speech stems; ffprobe measures
                            exact waveform duration to derive consumed simulation ticks.
                                         │
                                         ▼
 7. LEDGER SETTLEMENT       State deltas commit atomically to SQLite; Merkle root
                            seals; clock brake releases and simulation resumes.
```

#### 1. The Autonomous Clockwork Run

- **Discrete Logical Clock:** The simulation runner advances logical time by injecting discrete tick events into the engine pipeline. System wall clocks are strictly banned to ensure 100% bit-level reproducibility across execution environments.
- **Zero-CPU Dormancy:** Remote regions and unobserved rooms sleep in "The Void," consuming zero CPU cycles. Characters traveling along connecting corridors have their movement calculated analytically via continuous mathematical splines.
- **Accumulator Swell:** Within active hot-sets, physical fatigue, metabolic strain, doom meters in paranoia clocks, and social resentment in grievance ledgers accumulate monotonically in integer basis points ($0\text{ to }10{,}000\text{ bps}$).

#### 2. The Dramatic Halt & The Clock Brake

- **Axial Proximity Detection:** A continuous spatial sweep over the hex lattice evaluates physical coordinates. When opposing actors occupy identical or adjacent hexes ($d_{\text{axial}} \le 1$) within the same room boundary, an encounter becomes physically possible.
- **The Resentment Ceiling Breach:** The engine evaluates the dyadic tension accumulator between co-present entities. When current tension meets or exceeds the maximum boiling threshold ($I_{\text{curr}} \ge I_{\text{max}}$), the system triggers an emergency override.
- **Engaging the Brake:** The world's autonomous clockwork loop freezes instantly at Tick $t$. Mutex locks engage across the active world slice in SQLite, preventing background drift or concurrent state mutations while the encounter is resolved.

#### 3. Mechanical Pre-Resolution (The Opposed Tabletop Duel)

- **Adjudication Before Prose:** Before any dialogue or stage action is authored, the engine evaluates the conflict using strict tabletop rules:
  $$
  \text{Outcome} = 2d6_{\text{opposed}} + \text{Directional Leverage Strings} - \text{Somatic Debuffs}
  $$
- **Discrete Outcome Tiers:** The differential between rolls maps to an unambiguous resolution category: `DECISIVE_WIN`, `COSTLY_WIN`, `STANDOFF`, or `HUMILIATING_LOSS`.
- **The ScenePlan Intermediate Representation (IR):** The engine compiles an immutable intermediate contract. This contract specifies the victor, the defeated party, the physical trauma inflicted (such as muscle strain or broken bones), the transfer of physical inventory, and the mandatory **Required Exit Condition** (such as one character surrendering a tool or fleeing the room).

#### 4. The Authoring Pause & Spatial Stage Header Ingress

- **Mounting the Workspace:** The engine mounts the writing environment—either locally on disk inside the engine drafts directory, via cooked-mode terminal suspension in an external editor, or inside the web-native Writer's Desk (`writer.astro`).
- **Spatial Stage Header Injection:** The engine injects an immutable metadata header directly into Line 0 of the screenplay draft:
  - *Spatial Bounds:* Physical room identification and integer axial coordinates.
  - *Atmospheric Envelope:* Lighting levels in lux, ambient acoustic floor in centi-decibels ($\text{cdB}$), and the locked status of doors or portals.
  - *Somatic Capability Masks:* Physical capacity constraints for each actor, such as available arm count and locomotion tiers.
  - *The Mandated Exit State:* The explicit physical outcome that the scene is required to dramatize.
- **Dual Ingress Pathways:**
  - *Pathway A (Human Showrunner):* The human author drafts screenplay text in native `.fountain` syntax to fit the mandated exit condition. Authors can stamp an explicit `@lifecycle: locked` pragma into the header, activating the "Writer's Eject Button" to prevent automated agents or background passes from overwriting hand-crafted prose.
  - *Pathway B (Local Untrusted Model):* A local language model generates candidate lines on local CPU/RAM. The prompt is assembled using a Tripartite Attention Sandwich: dialect rules and voice exemplars in the top 15%, preceding narrative cadence in the middle 70%, and the active Tick $t$ State Header in the bottom 15% to force compliance with physical limits.

#### 5. The Compile-Time Invariant Gauntlet ($T_0 = \$0.00$)

Before candidate screenplay prose is permitted to advance to speech synthesis or media assembly, it passes through an in-memory validator that evaluates physical, biological, and epistemic boundaries at zero computational dollar cost:

- **Somatic Satisfiability (SAT) Solver:** Asserts that actions described in the script satisfy physical capability vectors:
  $$
  \sum \text{Held Items} \le \text{Arms Available}
  $$
  If a character with a severed arm or occupied hands attempts a two-handed task, the compiler trips an immediate somatic breach.
- **Banned Kinetic Verb Masks:** Descriptive stage directions and motor actions are extracted into a discrete token array and checked via set intersection:
  $$
  \text{Extracted Motor Actions} \cap \text{Banned Kinetic Verbs} = \emptyset
  $$
  If an actor with an injured limb executes a prohibited motion, execution halts immediately.
- **The Epistemic Horizon (Ignorance by Omission):** Characters are strictly bounded by their subjective belief ledgers. Any world fact, conspiracy, or distant event that has not physically entered an actor's knowledge register is scrubbed from the authoring context. If a character utters an unearned narrative secret, the compiler rejects the scene.
- **The Machine Rail vs. Voice Rail Partition:** The human-readable `.fountain` prose (the Voice Rail) governs literary subtext, cadence, and tone. The compiler extracts this prose into a structured `dialogue_ast.json` (the Machine Rail), which maps Dwight Swain Motivation-Reaction Units (MRUs), parentheticals, and discrete motor verbs. An automated verbatim parity gate asserts character-for-character equality between the spoken dialogue in the screenplay and the text in the AST.

#### 6. Time Measurement & Acoustic Clock Quantization

- **Screenplay Text Has Zero Inherent Time:** In this architecture, written prose cannot guess its own temporal duration; one page of script does not equal one minute of screen time.
- **Acoustic Synthesis:** Spoken dialogue lines from the verified AST are synthesized into uncompressed 48 kHz `.wav` audio stems using a local text-to-speech service.
- **Microsecond Waveform Probing:** The engine evaluates the synthesized audio files with media probing tools to calculate exact duration down to the millisecond ($D_{\text{ms}}$).
- **Dual Temporal Quantization:** The total acoustic duration of dialogue stems, added to mandatory physical pauses and Foley impacts, is quantized into two locked timebases:
  $$
  \text{Master Presentation Frames} = \left\lceil \frac{D_{\text{ms}}}{1000} \times 24 \right\rceil
  $$
  $$
  \Delta_{\text{ticks}} = \left\lceil \frac{D_{\text{ms}}}{1000 \times \text{Tick Interval}} \right\rceil
  $$
  The physical sound wave acts as the master clock of the universe: the duration of spoken speech dictates visual cut boundaries and determines exactly how many simulation ticks have elapsed.

#### 7. Atomic State Settlement & Ledger Resumption

- **Committing the Delta:** The physical consequences mandated by the ScenePlan IR and dramatized in the script (inflicted injuries, spent debt strings, transferred items, and updated door locks) are committed in an atomic transaction to the dedicated SQLite database (`data/sqlite/<series-slug>.local.db`).
- **Sealing the Cryptographic Merkle Root:** The engine recalculates the composite state hash using RFC 8785 canonicalization and SHA-256 digesting:
  $$
  H_{t+1} = \text{SHA-256}(H_t \parallel \text{Event Data})
  $$
- **Releasing the Clock Brake:** The master simulation clock advances forward by the exact number of consumed ticks:
  $$
  t \leftarrow t + \Delta_{\text{ticks}}
  $$
- **Waking Dormant Sites:** Background locations that slept in "The Void" during the authoring pause execute closed-form algebraic catch-up formulas ($f(\Delta_{\text{ticks}})$) to update environmental decay, resource consumption, and regional travel.
- **Resuming Autonomous Ticking:** Mutex locks disengage, and the simulation returns to autonomous fast-forward execution until the next dramatic threshold trips the clock brake.

### The Governance Firewall: Eliminating Causality Drift

To prevent human writers and automated drafting tools from corrupting long-term continuity, the pipeline enforces strict boundaries at the editorial interface (`SceneReviewGate.tsx`):

- **The Forbidden Patch Loop:** If a human editor encounters an unsatisfactory dialogue line or an awkward beat, opening the `.fountain` file to manually edit the text is strictly forbidden. Direct line editing severs narrative causality: the database loses track of *why* the character spoke, causing character psychology, downstream memory records, and future tabletop duels to permanently diverge from what was depicted on screen.
- **The Architectural Ratification Loop:** Instead of patching words directly, the showrunner exercises total creative control within a 60-second review window by operating on meaning constraints. The director selects an explicit diagnostic error code from a closed failure taxonomy (such as `UNEARNED_RELEASE` or `SOMATIC_CONTRADICTION`) or adjusts dynamic tension sliders (such as Curtness, Pacing, Threat Level, or Subtext Suppression). The simulation engine updates the underlying prompt envelope and regenerates the scene expression from verified state, keeping the event ledger, causal graph, and screenplay completely synchronized across multi-year narrative arcs.

### 1. What `000.series-sower-engine` Currently Has

In the active engine codebase (`packages/300.engine`):

- **Plain Integer Coordinates Only:** The engine tracks locations purely as raw integers $(q, r)$ stored in the `entities_live` SQLite table.
- **No Geometric Raycasting or Acoustic BFS:** The engine does not evaluate line-of-sight raycasts, structural wall occlusion, or centi-decibel ($\text{cdB}$) acoustic wavefront decay.
- **Dependency Manifest:** The engine links to `@camp_candor/001.lore` (for compiled universe state ingestion), but has no dependency declared for `@goblin/spatial-core`, `@goblin/spatial-types`, or `@camp_candor/001.spatial`.

### 2. The Current Architectural Status of `goblin-spatial`

Across the design history and project records, `goblin-spatial` exists primarily as an architectural blueprint, test specification, and separate prototype:

- **"Ready to be built, not yet ready to be trusted":** The consolidated synthesis notes that while the pure math concepts (doubled axial lattice, 8,190-pair supercover oracle, acoustic BFS) are decided, the execution bridge between `worker-sower-engine` and `goblin-spatial` remained unimplemented in the running engine.
- **"Internal Module First" Recommendation:** The original recommendation was to build spatial operations as an internal module inside the engine first (e.g., `10.location.unit` or `sower-engine/spatial/`) before extracting it into a standalone repository (`000.goblin-spatial`).

### 3. What Needs to Be Built to Connect Them

To establish live communication between `000.series-sower-engine` and `000.goblin-spatial`, the following infrastructure must be implemented:

1. **Dependency Link:** Add `@camp_candor/001.spatial` (or `@goblin/spatial-core`) to `packages/300.engine/package.json`.
2. **`SpatialPort` Implementation:** Author the synchronous `InProcessAdapter` in `300.engine` (typically inside `10.location.unit` or a dedicated spatial port unit) that conforms to the pure query contracts (`findPathBatch`, `evaluatePerception`).
3. **The Sanitizing Epistemic Firewall:** Add the query serializer that scrubs all character names, emotions, and narrative variables down to pure coordinates, dynamic door bitmasks (`SpatialOverlaySnapshot`), and integer movement budgets before passing them to the spatial library.
4. **Cryptographic Receipt Logging:** Update `event_log` in `02.database.unit` to persist the spatial verification quadruple (`topology_hash`, `overlay_hash`, `query_hash`, `receipt_hash`) whenever an actor moves or an event is observed.

In software project management, Epics define large-scale architectural initiatives, Features represent distinct functional capabilities delivered to the system, and Tasks are the atomic, non-overlapping engineering work items required to build them.

Following strict project categorization rules, every item related to establish live communication between 000.series-sower-engine and 000.goblin-spatial is decomposed below into single-responsibility Epics, Features, and Tasks with zero informational redundancy, explicit Git repository mappings, and zero use of the word "AND" in any title.

### Gemini

# Blueprint: [Epic 1] Zero-Direct-Ingress Spatial Boundary Implementation

## Target Repository: `000.goblin-spatial`

## Target Package: `packages/301.spatial`

### Architectural Core Overview

This blueprint specifies the engineering roadmap for implementing **[Epic 1] Zero-Direct-Ingress Spatial Boundary Implementation** within the `000.goblin-spatial` repository. The spatial subsystem functions as a stateless, pure mathematical oracle that evaluates physical possibility—including line-of-sight raycasts, acoustic wavefront propagation, and budget-bounded traversal—without owning mutable state, tracking character identities, or touching database connections.

The architecture enforces an asymmetric execution boundary:

1. **Synchronous Purity Across SpatialPort:** All geometric evaluations execute as pure, in-process function calls inside the host V8 isolate with zero Promises, zero `async`/`await`, and zero network hops, completing in nanoseconds to prevent event-loop descheduling.
2. **Zero Direct Ingress (The Epistemic Sanitization Filter):** The spatial engine strictly rejects any payload containing narrative context, character lore, emotional markers, or somatic injury descriptions. It accepts exclusively packed integer coordinates, structural bitmasks, and scalar cost limits.
3. **Cryptographic Proof Envelopes:** Every execution emits an immutable plain-data receipt sealed with a four-part audit quadruple ($H_{\text{topology}}, H_{\text{overlay}}, H_{\text{query}}, H_{\text{receipt}}$) to guarantee bit-identical historical replayability.
4. **Local Isolation First, Edge Staging Second:** Development, schema verification, and Blessed terminal controls operate locally inside unit containers before compiling into the Cloudflare Worker edge harness in `apps/worker`.

### Phase 1: Pure Geometric Types Validation In 38.types.unit

#### Task 1.1: Axial Coordinate Schema Definition

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/38.types.unit/fce/manifest.schema.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/38.types.unit/val/manifest.model.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/38.types.unit/types.test.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Define `AxialCoordSchema` via Zod enforcing strictly discrete integers for axial dimensions $q$ and $r$ (`z.number().int()`).
  - Author the doubled integer axial transform $s = -q - r$ to guarantee the planar zero-sum invariant ($q + r + s = 0$) with zero floating-point division.
  - Export the branded type `PackedAxial` representing an unsigned 32-bit integer bitmask:
    $$
    \text{PackedAxial} = (((q + 32768) \ \& \ \text{0xFFFF}) \ll 16) \ \vert{} \ ((r + 32768) \ \& \ \text{0xFFFF}) \ggg 0
    $$
  - Enforce `.strict()` on all coordinate schemas to reject untyped runtime properties.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - Floating-point coordinates are strictly prohibited; all spatial geometry operates on integer coordinates to eliminate cross-platform precision drift.

#### Task 1.2: Topology Manifest Schema Isolation

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/38.types.unit/fce/manifest.schema.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/38.types.unit/val/manifest.model.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Author `TopologicalCellSchema` requiring integer coordinates $(q, r)$ and a positive integer base traversal cost (`costUnits: z.number().int().positive()`).
  - Author `PortalEdgeSchema` modeling room-to-room transitions with `portalId`, `fromRoomId`, `toRoomId`, entry coordinates, exit coordinates, acoustic damping in centi-decibels (`acousticDampingCdb: z.number().int().nonnegative()`), and default traversal flags.
  - Author `OccluderDefinitionSchema` specifying structural line-of-sight barriers with `blocksLoSDefault: z.boolean()` and acoustic attenuation values.
  - Construct `LocationNodeSchema` and `TopologyManifestSchema` enforcing SHA-256 root hash verification (`/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/`) over static room collections.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - Static topology is content-addressed and immutable; once compiled by canon tooling, it remains read-only during all runtime evaluations.

#### Task 1.3: Dynamic Overlay Delta Validation

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/38.types.unit/fce/manifest.schema.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/38.types.unit/buz/overlay.buzz.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Author `DynamicOverlaySchema` with `.strict()` Zod constraints to represent ephemeral room states:
    - `overlayId`: String identifier.
    - `occluderOverrides`: Dictionary mapping canonical occluder IDs to boolean open/closed states (`Record<string, boolean>`).
    - `portalOverrides`: Dictionary mapping portal IDs to boolean traversable states.
    - `attenuationDeltasCdb`: Dictionary mapping barrier IDs to signed integer acoustic attenuation adjustments in centi-decibels.
  - Implement an in-memory overlay merger that superimposes dynamic deltas onto the static topology manifest during query execution and discards the combined snapshot immediately upon return.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - The dynamic overlay is a transient, stateless input parameter; the spatial core never retains mutable world state between function calls.

### Phase 2: Synchronous Spatial Port Interface In 14.port.unit

#### Task 2.1: Strict Scrubbed Query Schemas Creation

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/14.port.unit/fce/port.interface.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/14.port.unit/port.action.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/14.port.unit/buz/port.buzz.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Define `StrictRaycastQuerySchema` with `queryType: z.literal('RAYCAST')`, origin coordinates, target coordinates, and evaluation modes (`SUBTRACTIVE_LINEAR` or `MULTIPLICATIVE_BEER_LAMBERT`).
  - Define `StrictRoutingQuerySchema` with `queryType: z.literal('BOUNDED_PATH')`, origin coordinates, destination coordinates, integer movement budgets (`maxBudgetCostUnits`), and an integer work limit (`workEnvelopeLimit: z.number().int().positive().max(2048).default(512)`).
  - Define `StrictReachabilityQuerySchema` and `StrictDisplacementQuerySchema` with integer budgets and capacity limits.
  - Export `SpatialQueryDiscriminatorSchema` using `z.discriminatedUnion('queryType', [...])` to enforce compile-time exhaustiveness checking.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - Every query schema enforces `.strict()`, rejecting payloads containing unrecognized parameters or narrative metadata.

#### Task 2.2: Spatial Port Synchronous Execution Signature

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/14.port.unit/fce/port.interface.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/14.port.unit/port.model.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Declare the core `SpatialPort` interface without URLs, timeouts, or Promises:
    ```typescript
    export interface SpatialPort {
      readonly kernelVersion: string;
      invokeSynchronous<T = unknown>(
        rawQuery: unknown,
        context?: SpatialExecutionContext
      ): SynchronousExecutionReceipt<T>;
    }
    ```
    ```
  - Define `SpatialExecutionContext` accepting optional root hashes (`topologyHash`, `overlayHash`), the active logical tick, and an execution stream identifier.
  - Ban asynchronous callback patterns across all query execution paths within the port boundary.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - `SpatialPort` signatures must remain strictly synchronous; asynchronous Promises are forbidden on the geometric hot path to protect the V8 event loop.

#### Task 2.3: Zero Direct Ingress Narrative Stripping Filter

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/14.port.unit/buz/port.buzz.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/14.port.unit/port.test.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Author `sanitizeSpatialQueryPayload<T>(rawPayload: unknown): ScrubbedSpatialQuery`.
  - Inspect incoming objects and reject any containing forbidden narrative keys (`actorId`, `characterName`, `dialogue`, `trauma`, `emotion`, `inventory`).
  - Map unscrubbed external input errors directly to a fatal validation failure (`SCHEMA_BREACH`), halting execution before reaching the geometry kernel.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - The epistemic firewall guarantees that narrative identity cannot cross into the spatial kernel; calculations evaluate only geometry and physics.

#### Task 2.4: Integer Work Budget Tripwire Guard

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/14.port.unit/buz/port.buzz.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Author the execution envelope monitor that enforces `workEnvelopeLimit` (defaulting to 512 cells expanded).
  - Track search step increments inside traversal loops; if expanded nodes exceed the limit, interrupt execution immediately.
  - Emit an explicit `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` failure receipt rather than allowing unconstrained loops to trigger host process timeouts.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - Algorithmic complexity is bounded by discrete integer step budgets, converting CPU stalls into deterministic simulation outcomes.

### Phase 3: Cryptographic Audit Quadruple Ledger In 19.ledger.unit

#### Task 3.1: SHA256 Hash Digest Schema Authoring

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/19.ledger.unit/fce/ledger.interface.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/19.ledger.unit/val/ledger.model.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/19.ledger.unit/ledger.test.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Author `Sha256HashSchema` asserting a lowercase hexadecimal string of exactly 64 characters (`/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/`).
  - Define constant `GENESIS_PREFIX = "GOBLIN-SPATIAL-GENESIS::"` to seed baseline verification chains.
  - Enforce schema validation over all hash references throughout the ledger unit.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - Cryptographic hash representations must remain uniformly formatted across all platforms without string truncation.

#### Task 3.2: Spatial Audit Quadruple Contract Definition

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/19.ledger.unit/fce/ledger.interface.ts`
  - `packages/301.spatial/19.ledger.unit/val/ledger.model.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Define interface and Zod schema for `SpatialAuditQuadruple`:
    ```typescript
    export interface SpatialAuditQuadruple {
      readonly topologyHash: string;
      readonly overlayHash: string;
      readonly queryHash: string;
      readonly receiptHash: string;
    }
    ```
    ```
  - Author `SpatialLedgerEntrySchema` binding monotonic sequence identifiers, logical tick counters, stream IDs, query types, the audit quadruple, the kernel version string, and the resulting chained event hash.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - Every spatial interaction must produce an immutable audit quadruple, allowing any calculated result to be verified offline without re-running geometry routines.

#### Task 3.3: Deterministic Canonical JSON Digest Generator

- **Target Files:**
  - `packages/301.spatial/19.ledger.unit/buz/ledger.buzz.ts`
- **Technical Implementation:**
  - Implement an in-memory RFC 8785 JSON Canonicalization Scheme (JCS) serializer that sorts object keys lexicographically before hashing.
  - Compute SHA-256 digests over canonicalized strings using native Web Crypto or Node crypto primitives.
  - Exclude non-deterministic execution telemetry (e.g., wall-clock duration in microseconds or memory heap allocations) from the calculation of `receiptHash`.
- **Architectural Invariant:**
  - Identical input tuples $(\text{topology}, \text{overlay}, \text{query})$ must yield bit-identical receipt hashes across all execution environments.

### Phase 4: Sealed Plain Data Receipts In 42.receipt.unit

#### 1. `packages/301.spatial/38.types.unit/fce/manifest.schema.ts`

```typescript
import { z } from 'zod';
import { CentiDecibelsSchema } from './acoustic.schema.js';
import { Sha256HashSchema, PortalIdSchema, LocationIdSchema } from './ident.schema.js';

export const AXIAL_COORD_MIN = -32768;
export const AXIAL_COORD_MAX = 32767;

/**
 * Strict 2D Axial Hex Coordinate Schema.
 * Enforces exact integer values within [-32768, 32767] and planar cubic invariant q + r + s === 0.
 */
export const AxialCoordSchema = z
  .object({
    q: z
      .number()
      .int({ message: '[FLOAT_PROHIBITED] Coordinate q must be an integer' })
      .min(AXIAL_COORD_MIN, { message: '[OUT_OF_BOUNDS] Coordinate q underflows -32768' })
      .max(AXIAL_COORD_MAX, { message: '[OUT_OF_BOUNDS] Coordinate q overflows 32767' }),
    r: z
      .number()
      .int({ message: '[FLOAT_PROHIBITED] Coordinate r must be an integer' })
      .min(AXIAL_COORD_MIN, { message: '[OUT_OF_BOUNDS] Coordinate r underflows -32768' })
      .max(AXIAL_COORD_MAX, { message: '[OUT_OF_BOUNDS] Coordinate r overflows 32767' }),
    s: z
      .number()
      .int({ message: '[FLOAT_PROHIBITED] Coordinate s must be an integer' })
      .min(AXIAL_COORD_MIN * 2, { message: '[OUT_OF_BOUNDS] Coordinate s underflows' })
      .max(AXIAL_COORD_MAX * 2, { message: '[OUT_OF_BOUNDS] Coordinate s overflows' }),
  })
  .strict()
  .refine((data) => data.q + data.r + data.s === 0, {
    message: '[CUBIC_INVARIANT_BREACH] Planar coordinates must satisfy q + r + s === 0',
  });

export type AxialCoord = z.infer<typeof AxialCoordSchema>;

/**
 * Branded 32-bit unsigned integer packed axial cell identifier.
 */
export type PackedAxial = number & { readonly __brand: unique symbol };

export function packAxialCell(q: number, r: number): PackedAxial {
  if (!Number.isInteger(q) || !Number.isInteger(r)) {
    throw new TypeError('[FLOAT_PROHIBITED] Coordinates must be exact integers');
  }
  if (q < AXIAL_COORD_MIN || q > AXIAL_COORD_MAX || r < AXIAL_COORD_MIN || r > AXIAL_COORD_MAX) {
    throw new RangeError('[OUT_OF_BOUNDS] Coordinate outside [-32768, 32767]');
  }
  const qOffset = (q + 32768) & 0xffff;
  const rOffset = (r + 32768) & 0xffff;
  return (((qOffset << 16) | rOffset) >>> 0) as PackedAxial;
}

export function unpackAxialCell(packed: number): AxialCoord {
  if (!Number.isInteger(packed) || packed < 0 || packed > 0xffffffff) {
    throw new TypeError('[INVALID_PACKED_CELL] PackedAxial must be an unsigned 32-bit integer');
  }
  const qOffset = (packed >>> 16) & 0xffff;
  const rOffset = packed & 0xffff;
  const q = qOffset - 32768;
  const r = rOffset - 32768;
  const s = -q - r;
  return Object.freeze({ q, r, s });
}

export const TopologicalCellSchema = z
  .object({
    coord: AxialCoordSchema,
    packedId: z.number().int().nonnegative(),
    costUnits: z.number().int({ message: '[FLOAT_PROHIBITED] Traversal cost must be integer' }).positive(),
    passable: z.boolean(),
  })
  .strict();

export type TopologicalCell = z.infer<typeof TopologicalCellSchema>;

export const PortalEdgeSchema = z
  .object({
    portalId: PortalIdSchema,
    fromRoomId: LocationIdSchema,
    toRoomId: LocationIdSchema,
    entryCoord: AxialCoordSchema,
    exitCoord: AxialCoordSchema,
    acousticDampingCdb: CentiDecibelsSchema,
    traversableDefault: z.boolean(),
  })
  .strict();

export type PortalEdge = z.infer<typeof PortalEdgeSchema>;

export const OccluderDefinitionSchema = z
  .object({
    occluderId: z.string().regex(/^occ_[a-z0-9_]+$/, { message: '[INVALID_ID] Must match ^occ_[a-z0-9_]+$' }),
    coord: AxialCoordSchema,
    blocksLoSDefault: z.boolean(),
    attenuationCdb: CentiDecibelsSchema,
  })
  .strict();

export type OccluderDefinition = z.infer<typeof OccluderDefinitionSchema>;

export const TopologyManifestSchema = z
  .object({
    topologyId: LocationIdSchema,
    schemaVersion: z.string().regex(/^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$/, { message: '[INVALID_VERSION] Version must match ^v[0-9]+\\.[0-9]+$' }),
    merkleRootHex: Sha256HashSchema,
    cells: z.array(TopologicalCellSchema),
    portals: z.array(PortalEdgeSchema),
    occluders: z.array(OccluderDefinitionSchema),
  })
  .strict();

export type TopologyManifest = z.infer<typeof TopologyManifestSchema>;
```

import { z } from 'zod';
import { Sha256HashSchema } from './ident.schema.js';

/**
 * Ephemeral Dynamic Overlay Delta Schema.
 * Superimposed onto static topology during query execution.
 */
export const DynamicOverlaySchema = z
  .object({
    overlayId: z.string().min(1, { message: '[INVALID_ID] Overlay ID cannot be empty' }),
    parentTopologyHash: Sha256HashSchema,
    occluderOverrides: z.record(
      z.string().regex(/^occ_[a-z0-9_]+$/),
      z.boolean()
    ),
    portalOverrides: z.record(
      z.string().regex(/^portal_[a-z0-9_]+$/),
      z.boolean()
    ),
    attenuationDeltasCdb: z.record(
      z.string(),
      z.number().int({ message: '[FLOAT_PROHIBITED] Attenuation delta must be integer cdB' })
    ),
  })
  .strict();

export type DynamicOverlay = z.infer<typeof DynamicOverlaySchema>;

export interface MergedTopologySnapshot {
  readonly topologyId: string;
  readonly overlayId: string;
  readonly cells: readonly {
    readonly q: number;
    readonly r: number;
    readonly s: number;
    readonly costUnits: number;
    readonly passable: boolean;
  }[];
  readonly portals: readonly {
    readonly portalId: string;
    readonly fromRoomId: string;
    readonly toRoomId: string;
    readonly entryCoord: { readonly q: number; readonly r: number; readonly s: number };
    readonly exitCoord: { readonly q: number; readonly r: number; readonly s: number };
    readonly acousticDampingCdb: number;
    readonly traversable: boolean;
  }[];
  readonly occluders: readonly {
    readonly occluderId: string;
    readonly coord: { readonly q: number; readonly r: number; readonly s: number };
    readonly blocksLoS: boolean;
    readonly attenuationCdb: number;
  }[];
}
```

#### 3. `packages/301.spatial/38.types.unit/buz/overlay.buzz.ts`

```typescript
import type { TopologyManifest } from '../fce/manifest.schema.js';
import type { DynamicOverlay, MergedTopologySnapshot } from '../fce/overlay.schema.js';

/**
 * Pure in-memory overlay merger.
 * Superimposes dynamic deltas onto the static topology manifest without mutating source records.
 */
export function mergeOverlaySnapshot(
  manifest: TopologyManifest,
  overlay: DynamicOverlay
): MergedTopologySnapshot {
  const mergedPortals = manifest.portals.map((portal) => {
    const isOverridden = Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(overlay.portalOverrides, portal.portalId);
    const traversable = isOverridden ? overlay.portalOverrides[portal.portalId] : portal.traversableDefault;
    return Object.freeze({
      portalId: portal.portalId,
      fromRoomId: portal.fromRoomId,
      toRoomId: portal.toRoomId,
      entryCoord: Object.freeze({ ...portal.entryCoord }),
      exitCoord: Object.freeze({ ...portal.exitCoord }),
      acousticDampingCdb: portal.acousticDampingCdb,
      traversable,
    });
  });

  const mergedOccluders = manifest.occluders.map((occ) => {
    const hasLoSOverride = Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(overlay.occluderOverrides, occ.occluderId);
    const blocksLoS = hasLoSOverride ? overlay.occluderOverrides[occ.occluderId] : occ.blocksLoSDefault;

    const deltaCdb = overlay.attenuationDeltasCdb[occ.occluderId] ?? 0;
    const finalAttenuation = Math.max(0, occ.attenuationCdb + deltaCdb);

    return Object.freeze({
      occluderId: occ.occluderId,
      coord: Object.freeze({ ...occ.coord }),
      blocksLoS,
      attenuationCdb: finalAttenuation,
    });
  });

  const mergedCells = manifest.cells.map((cell) =>
    Object.freeze({
      q: cell.coord.q,
      r: cell.coord.r,
      s: cell.coord.s,
      costUnits: cell.costUnits,
      passable: cell.passable,
    })
  );

  return Object.freeze({
    topologyId: manifest.topologyId,
    overlayId: overlay.overlayId,
    cells: Object.freeze(mergedCells),
    portals: Object.freeze(mergedPortals),
    occluders: Object.freeze(mergedOccluders),
  });
}
```
